# The L3 application dispatch's release — the edit order (2026-11-14)

**What this file is.** The working file for step 2's order (entry 767 §3 step 2; entry 886 §2 item 5 and §3 step 1). One
row per dispatch PASSAGE: its dispatch lines, its opening words as the site table quotes them, every site-table entry
and overlap note bearing on it, and whether the change adds or removes lines (only such a change moves the lines below
it). Rows stand in DESCENDING order of dispatch line, the order the edits are applied in; each later slice inserts its
rows at their places by hand, so no mechanical sort is needed if that holds. **It decides nothing and edits nothing.**
Working practice only; no algorithm change; no documentation of the specification changed.

**Sources and their standing.** Every row is taken from the site table
(`ratification_surfaces/cowork_l3_application_release_site_table_2026_11_12.md`, 751,224 bytes, 6,905 lines as it stood when the rows were taken; 751,491 bytes and 6,907 lines since 2026-11-15 12:42 (the words from "as it stood" added at release, step 5 part 4, 2026-11-17, by the sitting booted on entry 1074, on the release check's finding 41; nothing replaced)), read at
its entries by the sitting that wrote the row. The dispatch (`records/cc/instructions/cc_instruction_l3_application_2026_11_08.md`)
was not opened for this file: every dispatch line and quoted wording here is **[relayed from the site table]**, which
records each as checked at the dispatch at 593,140 bytes. Line numbers are the dispatch's at that size. Edits to the
working list (`ratification_surfaces/cowork_l3_application_release_points_2026_11_09.md`) are not dispatch edits; they
stand in their own section at the end.

**"Lines move"** says, from the block's own wording only: **adds lines** where the block orders a new item, paragraph,
form or table line; **inside its lines** where the block orders a pointer or a replacement and names no new line;
**not established** where the wording leaves it open. Kept former wordings may add lines where none is expected: step 2
checks each at the site before relying on this column.

       > flag stating whether that chord is diatonic in both tonalities. The flag is a fact, not a test of pivot-ness. No chord
       > is named "the pivot". Each rival whole reading L2 publishes gives its own set. Under IC-L2 a pivot is published as one
       > span with one tonality and the other tonality's reading as a rival (L2-S19); the label re-expresses L2's decided
       > chords and decides no tonality.**
